I love you, against I like you as a friend	je t’aime /ʒə tɛm/; je t’aime bien /ʒə tɛm bjɛ̃/: bien softens the verb with a person	french 1000words aimer challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::aimer — to like, to love::Challenges
Aimer /eme/ against j’aime /ʒɛm/: why two vowels?	ai is the closed /e/ at the end of the syllable and the open /ɛ/ before a consonant that is said	french 1000words aimer challenge sound	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::aimer — to like, to love::Challenges
I like chocolate: which article after aimer?	the definite one, always: j’aime le chocolat /ʒɛm lə ʃɔkɔla/, never du	french 1000words aimer challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::aimer — to like, to love::Challenges

The five forms of beau, with IPA	beau /bo/, bel /bɛl/, belle /bɛl/, beaux /bo/, belles /bɛl/: five spellings, two sounds	french 1000words beau challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::beau — beautiful::Challenges
When is the masculine bel used?	Before a masculine noun starting with a vowel or a mute h, in the singular: un bel appartement /œ̃ bɛl apaʁtəmɑ̃/, un bel homme /œ̃ bɛl ɔm/; after être it is beau: cet homme est beau /sɛt‿ɔm ɛ bo/	french 1000words beau challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::beau — beautiful::Challenges
Beautiful eyes: why de and not des, and what is the liaison?	Before a plural adjective that precedes its noun des becomes de, and the x is said as /z/: de beaux yeux /də boz‿jø/	french 1000words beau challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::beau — beautiful::Challenges

The forms of confortable, with IPA	confortable /kɔ̃fɔʁtabl/ for both genders, confortables /kɔ̃fɔʁtabl/ in the plural: an adjective already ending in -e adds no feminine -e, and the four are said alike	french 1000words confortable challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::confortable — comfortable::Challenges
Where does confortable go, before or after the noun?	After, as most adjectives do: une maison confortable /yn mɛzɔ̃ kɔ̃fɔʁtabl/; with a short adjective before it, une grande maison confortable /yn ɡʁɑ̃d mɛzɔ̃ kɔ̃fɔʁtabl/	french 1000words confortable challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::confortable — comfortable::Challenges
Can a person be confortable?	No: a chair, a bed, a house or a salary is confortable; a person is bien /bjɛ̃/, à l’aise /a lɛz/ or bien installé /bjɛ̃n‿ɛ̃stale/; je suis confortable is a Quebec anglicism	french 1000words confortable challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::confortable — comfortable::Challenges

The present of dormir, all six forms	je dors, tu dors, il dort, nous dormons, vous dormez, ils dorment /ʒə dɔʁ, ty dɔʁ, il dɔʁ, nu dɔʁmɔ̃, vu dɔʁme, il dɔʁm/	french 1000words dormir challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::dormir — to sleep::Challenges
Why je dors and not je dormis?	Dormir follows partir, not finir: the singular of the present drops the stem’s last consonant (dors, dort /dɔʁ/) and the plural keeps it (dormons /dɔʁmɔ̃/); finir inserts -ss- instead (nous finissons)	french 1000words dormir challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::dormir — to sleep::Challenges
Five verbs that conjugate like dormir, with the je form	partir, je pars /paʁ/; sortir, je sors /sɔʁ/; sentir, je sens /sɑ̃/; servir, je sers /sɛʁ/; mentir, je mens /mɑ̃/	french 1000words dormir challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::dormir — to sleep::Challenges

What happens to the d of grand before a vowel?	It is said as a /t/: un grand homme /œ̃ ɡʁɑ̃t‿ɔm/, un grand arbre /œ̃ ɡʁɑ̃t‿aʁbʁ/; in the plural the s gives /z/: de grands arbres /də ɡʁɑ̃z‿aʁbʁ/	french 1000words grand challenge sound	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::grand — big, tall::Challenges
Big houses: why de and not des?	Before a plural adjective that precedes its noun, careful French writes de: de grandes maisons /də ɡʁɑ̃d mɛzɔ̃/; after the noun des stays: des maisons confortables /de mɛzɔ̃ kɔ̃fɔʁtabl/	french 1000words grand challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::grand — big, tall::Challenges

Why is jardin /ʒaʁdɛ̃/ but jardiner /ʒaʁdine/?	At the end of a word -in is the nasal vowel /ɛ̃/ and no n is said; before a vowel the n is said and the vowel turns oral, as in cousin, cousine /kuzɛ̃, kuzin/	french 1000words jardin challenge sound	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::un jardin — a garden::Challenges

Why do la lie, le lit and il lit sound alike?	All three are /li/: the final -e and -t are silent. Is the c of sac said? And of blanc?	Yes, /sak/: a final c is said after an oral vowel (lac, parc, avec); silent after a nasal vowel, blanc /blɑ̃/, banc /bɑ̃/; tabac /taba/ and estomac /ɛstɔma/ are the exceptions	french 1000words sac challenge sound	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::un sac — a bag::Challenges

The four forms of tout, with IPA	tout /tu/, toute /tut/, tous /tu/ or /tus/, toutes /tut/	french 1000words tout challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::tout — all, every; the whole::Challenges
When is tous /tus/, with the s said?	As a pronoun, with no noun after it: ils sont tous là /il sɔ̃ tus la/, merci à tous /mɛʁsi a tus/; before a noun it is /tu/: tous les jours /tu le ʒuʁ/	french 1000words tout challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::tout — all, every; the whole::Challenges
Why toute seule but tout émue?	The adverb tout agrees only before a feminine adjective starting with a consonant, for the sound: toute seule /tut sœl/; before a vowel the liaison gives the /t/ anyway: tout émue /tut‿emy/	french 1000words tout challenge grammar	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::tout — all, every; the whole::Challenges
Three phrases with tout à: give them with IPA	tout à fait /tut‿a fɛ/ quite, tout à l’heure /tut‿a lœʁ/ a moment ago, tout à coup /tut‿a ku/ suddenly	french 1000words tout challenge words	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::tout — all, every; the whole::Challenges

tu or tout: which vowel?	tu /ty/ with the front rounded /y/, tout /tu/ with /u/; the lips stay rounded, the tongue moves	french 1000words tout challenge sound	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::tout — all, every; the whole::Challenges

vient or viennent: what happens to the nasal?	vient /vjɛ̃/ ends in the nasal vowel; viennent /vjɛn/ has an oral vowel and the n said, because a vowel follows in the spelling (denasalisation)	french 1000words venir challenge sound	French::1000 words (EN-FR)::venir — to come::Challenges
